- [ ] **Step 2:** `test/key-credentials-real-api.test.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * RFC-016 against the REAL Bridge API (not the stub): enrolment, mint, DPoP on
 * HTTP + WS, copy detection → lock, session revoke → reconnect=true, logout
 * verified-not-advanced. Same gate and harness shape as inbound-routing.test.ts:
 *
 *   BRIDGE_API_DIR            <bridge>/packages/api   (a checkout WITH the RFC-016 server slice)
 *   BRIDGE_TEST_PG_ADMIN_URL  a Postgres 18 for scratch databases
 *
 * ⚠️ SKIPS when either is missing — and then proves nothing. When they ARE set but
 * the server predates RFC-016, the first test FAILS on discovery (no
 * client_credentials) instead of skipping: a wired-up run against the wrong server
 * must be red, never quietly green.
 */
import { test, expect, describe, afterAll } from "bun:test";
import { mkdtempSync, cpSync, existsSync, rmSync } from "node:fs";
import { join } from "node:path";
import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
import { Client } from "@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/client/index.js";
import { StdioClientTransport } from "@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/client/stdio.js";
import { discover, supportsKeyCredentials, TokenClient } from "../auth/core/protocol";
import { Clock } from "../auth/core/clock";
import { softwareSigner } from "../auth/core/signer";
import { randomB64url } from "../auth/core/b64url";
import { readInstallation, readKey, readState } from "../auth/node/store";
import { withInstallationLock } from "../auth/node/lock";
import { joinStateSeq } from "../auth/core/join-state";

const API_DIR = process.env.BRIDGE_API_DIR ?? "";
const HAVE_SERVER = !!API_DIR && (await Bun.file(join(API_DIR, "src/index.ts")).exists().catch(() => false));
const ADMIN_URL = process.env.BRIDGE_TEST_PG_ADMIN_URL ?? "postgres://bridge:bridge-test-not-a-secret@127.0.0.1:5433/bridge";
async function pgReachable(): Promise<boolean> {
  try {
    const probe = new Bun.SQL(ADMIN_URL);
    await probe`SELECT 1`;
    await probe.close();
    return true;
  } catch {
    return false;
  }
}
const HAVE_PG = HAVE_SERVER ? await pgReachable() : false;
const SERVER_TS = join(import.meta.dir, "..", "server.ts");

let teardown: (() => Promise<void>) | null = null;
afterAll(async () => {
  if (teardown) await teardown();
});

/** A scratch database + a real API process, one agent `me` with an enrolment key (inbound-routing.test.ts's recipe). */
async function bootBridge() {
  const dbName = `bridge_plugin_keys_${Date.now()}_${process.pid}`;
  const admin = new Bun.SQL(ADMIN_URL);
  await admin.unsafe(`CREATE DATABASE "${dbName}"`);
  const dbUrl = ADMIN_URL.replace(/\/[^/?]+(\?|$)/, `/${dbName}$1`);
  process.env.DATABASE_URL = dbUrl;
  const api: any = await import(join(API_DIR, "src/db/index.ts"));
  await api.runMigrations();
  await api.db.insert(api.schema.agents).values([{ id: "me", name: "Me" }]);
  await api.pool.query(`UPDATE principals SET tenant_id = 'default' WHERE id = 'me'`);
  await api.pool.query(
    `INSERT INTO tenant_handles (tenant_id, principal_id, handle, folded_handle, created_at)
     VALUES ('default', 'me', 'me', 'me', extract(epoch from now())::bigint)`
  );
  const credsMod: any = await import(join(API_DIR, "src/agent-credentials.ts"));
  const k = await credsMod.mintEnrolmentKey("me", "default", { id: "me", type: "agent", contextId: null }, { maxUses: 5 });
  if (!k.ok) throw new Error(`mintEnrolmentKey: ${k.error}`);
  const PORT = String(4900 + Math.floor(Number(process.hrtime.bigint() % 90n)));
  const url = `http://localhost:${PORT}`;
  const server = Bun.spawn(["bun", join(API_DIR, "src/index.ts")], {
    env: {
      ...process.env,
      DATABASE_URL: dbUrl,
      PORT,
      BRIDGE_PUBLIC_URL: url,
      FORGE_OIDC_CLIENT_SECRET: "x",
      BETTER_AUTH_SECRET: "test-secret-at-least-32-characters-long",
      NODE_ENV: "test",
    },
    stdout: "ignore",
    stderr: "ignore",
  });
  let up = false;
  for (let i = 0; i < 80 && !up; i++) {
    try {
      up = (await fetch(`${url}/api/health`)).ok;
    } catch {}
    if (!up) await Bun.sleep(250);
  }
  teardown = async () => {
    server.kill();
    try {
      await api.closePool();
    } catch {}
    try {
      await admin.unsafe(`DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS "${dbName}" WITH (FORCE)`);
    } catch {}
    await admin.close().catch(() => {});
    teardown = null;
  };
  return { url, up, pool: api.pool, enrolmentKey: k.key as string };
}

async function plugin(url: string, dir: string, env: Record<string, string>) {
  const transport = new StdioClientTransport({
    command: "bun",
    args: [SERVER_TS],
    env: {
      ...process.env,
      BRIDGE_API_URL: url,
      BRIDGE_AUTOCONNECT: "1",
      BRIDGE_BROWSER: "none",
      BRIDGE_STATE_DIR: dir,
      CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA: dir,
      CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR: dir,
      CLAUDE_CODE_SSE_PORT: "",
      ...env,
    } as Record<string, string>,
  });
  const client = new Client({ name: "t", version: "0" }, { capabilities: {} });
  const notices: string[] = [];
  client.fallbackNotificationHandler = async (n: any) => {
    if (typeof n?.params?.content === "string") notices.push(n.params.content);
  };
  await client.connect(transport);
  const status = async () => JSON.parse(((await client.callTool({ name: "status", arguments: {} })) as any).content[0].text);
  return { client, notices, status, close: () => client.close().catch(() => {}) };
}

async function until(pred: () => boolean | Promise<boolean>, ms: number): Promise<boolean> {
  const end = Date.now() + ms;
  while (Date.now() < end) {
    if (await pred()) return true;
    await Bun.sleep(100);
  }
  return !!(await pred());
}

describe.skipIf(!HAVE_SERVER || !HAVE_PG)("RFC-016 against the real API", () => {
  test(
    "enrol → mint → WS + HTTP with DPoP; session revoke → connect (reconnect=true); a copy → LOCK; logout does not advance",
    async () => {
      const b = await bootBridge();
      expect(b.up, "bridge server did not start").toBe(true);
      expect(supportsKeyCredentials(await discover(b.url)), "server predates RFC-016 (no client_credentials)").toBe(true);
      const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "keys-real-"));
      const p1 = await plugin(b.url, dir, { BRIDGE_ENROLMENT_KEY: b.enrolmentKey, BRIDGE_SESSION_KEY: "real-s1" });
      try {
        // ── enrol + mint + WS auth with a proof ─────────────────────────────
        expect(await until(async () => (await p1.status()).websocket === "connected", 20_000), "never authenticated").toBe(true);
        const inst = readInstallation(dir)!.installationId;
        const row = async () =>
          (await b.pool.query(`SELECT js_seq, revoked_at, revoke_reason, jkt FROM agent_grants WHERE id = $1`, [inst])).rows[0];
        expect(Number((await row()).js_seq)).toBe(joinStateSeq(readState(dir)!)!);
        expect((await row()).jkt).toBe(readInstallation(dir)!.jkt);
        // ── HTTP with a proof ──────────────────────────────────────────────
        const lc: any = await p1.client.callTool({ name: "list_channels", arguments: {} });
        expect(lc.isError, JSON.stringify(lc.content)).not.toBe(true);

        // ── session revoke (scope=session, from this machine's own files) → 4008 → connect ──
        await withInstallationLock(dir, async () => {
          const meta = await discover(b.url);
          await new TokenClient(new Clock()).revoke(meta, await softwareSigner(readKey(dir)!), {
            installationId: inst,
            joinState: readState(dir)!,
            attempt: null,
            scope: "session",
            sessionKey: "real-s1",
          });
        });
        expect(await until(() => p1.notices.some((n) => n.includes("/bridge:connect")), 10_000)).toBe(true);
        await p1.client.callTool({ name: "connect", arguments: {} });
        expect(await until(async () => (await p1.status()).websocket === "connected", 20_000), "reconnect=true did not open a new session").toBe(true);

        // ── a COPY mints elsewhere; this machine's next mint is LOCKED ─────
        const loot = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "keys-loot-"));
        try {
          cpSync(dir, loot, { recursive: true });
          const meta = await discover(b.url);
          await new TokenClient(new Clock()).mint(meta, await softwareSigner(readKey(loot)!), {
            installationId: inst,
            joinState: readState(loot)!,
            attempt: randomB64url(32),
            sessionKey: "thief",
            reconnect: false,
          });
        } finally {
          rmSync(loot, { recursive: true, force: true });
        }
        // A second process of this machine starts and mints with the now-stale state.
        const dir2Env = { BRIDGE_SESSION_KEY: "real-s2" };
        const p2 = await plugin(b.url, dir, dir2Env);
        try {
          expect(await until(async () => (await row()).revoked_at !== null, 20_000), "copy was not detected").toBe(true);
          expect((await row()).revoke_reason).toBe("reuse_detected");
          expect(await until(() => p1.notices.some((n) => /locked/i.test(n)), 10_000), "p1 not told about the lock").toBe(true);
          expect(existsSync(join(dir, "key.json"))).toBe(false);
        } finally {
          await p2.close();
        }

        // ── logout on a fresh enrolment: revoked, js_seq NOT advanced ────────
        const dir3 = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "keys-real3-"));
        const p3 = await plugin(b.url, dir3, { BRIDGE_ENROLMENT_KEY: b.enrolmentKey, BRIDGE_SESSION_KEY: "real-s3" });
        try {
          expect(await until(async () => (await p3.status()).websocket === "connected", 20_000)).toBe(true);
          const inst3 = readInstallation(dir3)!.installationId;
          const seqBefore = Number((await b.pool.query(`SELECT js_seq FROM agent_grants WHERE id = $1`, [inst3])).rows[0].js_seq);
          const out: any = await p3.client.callTool({ name: "logout", arguments: {} });
          expect(out.content[0].text).toMatch(/revoked in Bridge/);
          const after = (await b.pool.query(`SELECT js_seq, revoked_at FROM agent_grants WHERE id = $1`, [inst3])).rows[0];
          expect(after.revoked_at).not.toBeNull();
          expect(Number(after.js_seq)).toBe(seqBefore);
        } finally {
          await p3.close();
          rmSync(dir3, { recursive: true, force: true });
        }
      } finally {
        await p1.close();
        rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
      }
    },
    180_000
  );
});
```

## Task 13: Release — publish order (after the server deploy)

`bored-marketplace` sources the plugin from this repo's `main`: **merging the PR IS publishing the code**. `marketplace.json` `version` is cosmetic but bumped per release.

1. **Server slice deployed first** (RFC-016 §6.1; its migration revokes every live grant, E13). Verify at the artifact: `curl -s https://bridge-api.jorgennilsson.net/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server/api/agent-auth | jq .grant_types_supported` contains `client_credentials` and not `refresh_token`.
2. Immediately merge the plugin PR (same day, §6.2). From this moment every 0.23 session is dead: its next refresh gets `unsupported_grant_type`, which 0.23 treats as a retryable network error ("Bridge sign-in failed: unsupported_grant_type (400)") forever — it will NOT say "update". Tell users out of band.
3. Bump `version` in `BoredDevelopers/bored-marketplace` `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` → `0.25.0`, push (after RFC-015's 0.24.0 if it ships first — the listing must never go backwards).
4. On each machine: `/plugin update bridge` (or marketplace update), restart the Claude sessions, then `/bridge:login` once per machine and per `BRIDGE_PROFILE` (the plugin's status says so). Headless machines: a fresh `BRIDGE_ENROLMENT_KEY` if the old key's uses are spent; spawned children are re-spawned by their owner (E13).
5. Verify at the artifact, not the marketplace: prod `SELECT DISTINCT client_version FROM agent_contexts WHERE …recent…` shows `0.25.0`; `agent_grants` rows for the machines have `jkt` + `js_seq > 0`; no `reuse_detected` in the first day (a false lock would show here).

If the plugin is merged before the server deploy by mistake: 0.25 says "this Bridge server does not support key credentials yet … must be upgraded" (discovery check) — it does not lock, delete or loop.

## Migration story (0.23 / 0.24 → 0.25)

| RFC-014 on disk (0.23; also RFC-015's 0.24 if it ships first) | What 0.25 does | Where |
|---|---|---|
| `<profile>/credentials.json` | `configError()` immediately: "Bridge plugin 0.25 signs in with a per-machine key (RFC-016); the sign-in from plugin 0.24 or earlier (RFC-014) cannot be carried over — run /bridge:login". At startup (after the session key settles) `retireLegacyCredentials()` deletes it under the lock and writes `upgrade-required.json` `{apiUrl, installationName, retiredAt}`. | `manager.ts`, `store.ts retireLegacy`, `server.ts` boot |
| `<profile>/sessions/<key>.json` (refresh tokens) | Deleted — only files whose JSON has `refreshToken`. The hook's `pid-*.json` / `sse-*.json` in the same directory survive; the directory is removed only if empty (named profiles). | `store.ts retireLegacy` |
| crash leftovers `credentials.json.<pid>.<ms>.tmp`, `sessions/<key>.json.<pid>.<ms>.tmp` (0.23's `writeJson` temp names) | Deleted. Matched by 0.23's exact pattern `\.json\.\d+\.\d+\.tmp$` — never the hook's `<file>.<pid>-<rand>.tmp` nor 0.25's own `writeAtomic` temps (guard S9). | `store.ts legacyFiles` |
| refresh-token files but NO `credentials.json` (it was deleted, or a crash) | Retired too: `hasLegacyCredentials()` is "any RFC-014 file", not "credentials.json exists"; the marker then has no `apiUrl`. | `store.ts legacyFiles` |
| retirement throws (EACCES, ENOSPC, …) | Logged; boot continues (enrolment key, connect); `configError()` still says "run /bridge:login"; the next start retries. | `server.ts` boot (W12) |
| `BRIDGE_API_URL` unset (0.23 used `credentials.json.apiUrl`) | `apiUrl()` falls back to the marker's `apiUrl`, so `/bridge:login` works without `/bridge:configure`. | `manager.ts apiUrl()` |
| `BRIDGE_ENROLMENT_KEY` in `.env` | Enrols a fresh key at startup (if the key has uses left), clearing the marker. | `enrolFromKeyIfNeeded` |
| first successful login/enrol | marker cleared; status clean. | `writeEnrolment` |

No server call is made for the old installation: the RFC-016 migration already revoked every grant (§4.1).

**Only the running process's profile is retired — deliberately.** Its RFC-014 grants are dead server-side (E13), so another profile's leftovers are no live credential; they are retired — with the "run /bridge:login" notice the person needs for THAT profile — the first time a session starts on it. A process does not reach into profiles it was not started for: their locks, their markers, possibly another API. (Retiring every profile at boot would only shorten how long dead tokens sit on disk; it is not worth a process touching directories outside its own profile.)

## RFC-016 §9 alignment (C1–C19)

| C | Client handling in this plan | Test / guard |
|---|---|---|
| C1 JSON everywhere | `postDpop` sends JSON (unchanged) | all stub suites |
| C2 `aud` string or 1-elem array | client always sends the issuer STRING; stub deliberately refuses arrays (stricter) | P5, ST6 |
| C3 `platform`/`client_version` optional on mint; `key_storage` on all 3 enrolments | sent on mint + all three enrolment grants | T6, auth-manager "first use mints" |
| C4 attempt = 32 random bytes b64url (43 chars) | `randomB64url(32)`; stub enforces `{22,128}` + `attempt_invalid` no lock; client treats `attempt_invalid` as unlisted ⇒ stop, keep files | S2/S3, N10 |
| C5 cap 64 per installation | server-side; stub models it per installation | "session_limit …", "at the session cap …" |
| C6 `key_already_enrolled` ⇒ fresh key, once | protocol never retries it with the same key; manager retries once with a fresh key (enrolment key, device) | N1, N2, N3 |
| C7 `dpop_jkt` mismatch ⇒ `invalid_grant`, burnt | stub models it; loopback therefore cannot use the C6 fresh-key retry (see below) | N11 |
| C8 device: proof on every poll | `pollDevice(poll)` with a fresh proof per poll; stub consumes pending-poll proofs | "device flow: … a fresh proof per poll" |
| C9 code replay ⇒ lock | server/stub only; client never replays (single exchange) | N12 |
| C10 alert recipients | server only | — |
| C11 revoke `200 {"ok":true}`; `installation_*` on logout = already revoked | `TokenClient.revoke` requires `ok:true`; logout maps `installation_gone` ⇒ revoked, except `installation_locked` ⇒ "a copy was used" (E8 must reach the person) | N4, M13/M14, M23 |
| C12 `agent_deactivated` ⇒ terminal, delete | `installation_gone` path | "C12: agent_deactivated …", M6 |
| C13 `assertion_invalid` ⇒ clock retry once, then stop, KEEP files; unlisted ⇒ stop, keep | `postDpop` one retry after `Date`; manager `clock`/`refused` keep files | T2/T3, N9 |
| C14 4001 ⇒ drop token, one immediate mint, then slow | `server.ts`: `invalidateAccess` + `scheduleReconnect(immediate)` once per authenticated socket (`remintedAfter4001`, re-armed on `authenticated`); after an in-band `reauth` the socket's recorded token/grant is the reauthed one, so a 4001 drops THAT token | W3, N5, N6, N7, W11 |
| C15 nonces HTTP only, never WS | WS proofs carry no nonce; stub never demands one on WS | "a resource nonce …", W6 |
| C16 `htu` HTTP = origin + path, WS = origin + `/ws` | `normalizeHtu(apiUrl + path)` (apiUrl is an origin, C16), `wsHtu` | P1, P2 |
| C17 `logout --local` warning | tool text + skill: STAYS ENROLLED until revoked in Settings → Agents → Machines | N8 |
| C18 0.23 told out of band | no code; Task 13 step 2 | — |
| C19 migration replay | server only | — |

**Residual found while aligning (client view):** C6 + C7 together mean a loopback login can never recover from `key_already_enrolled`: the code is bound to the first key's `dpop_jkt`, and redeeming it with a fresh key burns it. The plan does not retry on loopback; the person re-runs `/bridge:login` (a fresh key per run makes this practically unreachable: it needs a P-256 collision or a server bug).

**If the server verifies with `jose` instead of hand-rolled WebCrypto:** nothing in this plan changes. The wire format is fixed by RFC 7515/7523/9449 + RFC-016 (compact JWS, ES256 raw r‖s, `typ`s, claims, `htu`/`ath` rules), and the client already signs through WebCrypto and is verified here by an independent node:crypto verifier. `jose` accepts exactly that format; the only thing to re-check is that the server's `jose` config pins `algorithms: ["ES256"]` and `typ` (both are server-side). Task 9's real-API run is what proves it either way.

## Gaps / contradictions in RFC-016 (client view) — as raised before §9 (all now answered by C1–C19, kept for the record; where a line below says "Plan: …", the alignment table above supersedes it)

1. **WS DPoP nonce** — E12 says clients must handle `use_dpop_nonce` "so server nonces can be enabled later", but E11's `auth`/`reauth` frames have no way to learn a nonce (no header, no error frame, 4001 carries no nonce). If the server enables nonces for WS, every client breaks. Plan: WS proofs never carry a nonce; the stub never demands one on WS.
2. **`htu` for WS vs HTTP** — E11: normalised apiUrl ORIGIN + `/ws`; §3.4 HTTP: apiUrl + path. Identical today (`config.ts` makes apiUrl an origin), but a client configured with a path-prefixed `BRIDGE_API_URL` would disagree with itself. Plan follows the letter (`wsHtu` = origin + `/ws`).
3. **Mint body lacks `platform` / `client_version`** — RFC-014's session grant sent them and the server stores them on the installation (Machines UI). §3.3 omits them. Plan keeps sending them; the server must accept (or ignore) them.
4. **`key_storage` only on the enrolment-key grant** — §3.2 table lists it for `enrolment-key`; loopback/device are "as RFC-014 + DPoP". Plan sends `key_storage` on all three.
5. **`agent_deactivated` is terminal + delete key** (§3.3) — but deactivation is reversible (RFC-014 / today's 4003 "credential" class retries slowly so a reactivated agent recovers without touching the machine). After this release (0.25) a reactivated agent needs every machine re-enrolled. Also unspecified: does deactivation now close sockets 4003 or 4008?
6. **`assertion_invalid` "retry once; then terminal"** — terminal is not defined (delete key?). A clock problem is not a key problem; plan stops and KEEPS the files.
7. **Unlisted descriptions** — `invalid_client` / `invalid_grant` / `invalid_request` with a description not in the table: undefined. Plan: stop, keep files, show the code.
8. **Revoke answers** — §3.5 defines no success body and no error table; plan assumes `200 {}` and the §3.3 codes, and treats `installation_*` on logout as "already revoked".
9. **4001 under DPoP** — covers both a dead token and a bad proof; RFC doesn't say the client must re-mint. Plan invalidates the token on 4001 (so the next auth mints and learns the real reason) but keeps RFC-014's slow 60 s→5 min "credential" backoff.
10. **Device flow key binding** — no `dpop_jkt` at `device_authorization`; the key is whichever proof accompanies the successful poll. Every `authorization_pending` poll also carries a proof (single-use jti) — unspecified whether the server wants/validates them.
11. **"key already enrolled" = `invalid_dpop_proof`** — the same code the client is told to answer with "new proof, retry" (§3.3 row). Indistinguishable without matching the description; plan retries once then fails (a fresh key per login makes it practically unreachable).
12. **Token-endpoint encoding** — RFC 6749 says form-encoded; the plugin (and the server's Elysia routes) use JSON. Unspecified for the SDK / conformance suite.
13. **`attempt` format** — unspecified length/charset; plan sends 43-char base64url (256-bit). Server validation unknown.
14. **E13 message** — 0.23 gets `unsupported_grant_type` on `refresh_token`, which 0.23 reports as a retryable network failure, not "update". Nothing a client release can fix; needs an announcement.
15. **`logout local`** — RFC-016 doesn't mention it; it leaves the installation alive up to `expires_at` (residual ii). Plan keeps the option and says so in the tool text and skill.

## Unresolved questions

1. Loopback + `key_already_enrolled` (C6 vs C7): fail and re-run login — ok?
2. C14: immediate re-mint once per authenticated socket — right scope?
3. 0.25 vs RFC-015's 0.24: which merges first?
4. Enrolment cut at the lock deadline after the server already enrolled ⇒ orphan installation + a spent key use. Accept (rare) or revoke-on-timeout?
5. Retire only the running profile (chosen) — ok, or sweep all profiles at boot?
